=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of strings words and an integer k.
For each index i in the range [0, words.length - 1], find the length of the longest common prefix among any k strings (selected at distinct indices) from the remaining array after removing the i^th element.
Return an array answer, where answer[i] is the answer for i^th element. If removing the i^th element leaves the array with fewer than k strings, answer[i] is 0.
 
Example 1:

Input: words = ["jump","run","run","jump","run"], k = 2
Output: [3,4,4,3,4]
Explanation:

Removing index 0 ("jump"):

	
words becomes: ["run", "run", "jump", "run"]. "run" occurs 3 times. Choosing any two gives the longest common prefix "run" (length 3).


Removing index 1 ("run"):
	
words becomes: ["jump", "run", "jump", "run"]. "jump" occurs twice. Choosing these two gives the longest common prefix "jump" (length 4).


Removing index 2 ("run"):
	
words becomes: ["jump", "run", "jump", "run"]. "jump" occurs twice. Choosing these two gives the longest common prefix "jump" (length 4).


Removing index 3 ("jump"):
	
words becomes: ["jump", "run", "run", "run"]. "run" occurs 3 times. Choosing any two gives the longest common prefix "run" (length 3).


Removing index 4 ("run"):
	
words becomes: ["jump", "run", "run", "jump"]. "jump" occurs twice. Choosing these two gives the longest common prefix "jump" (length 4).




Example 2:

Input: words = ["dog","racer","car"], k = 2
Output: [0,0,0]
Explanation:

Removing any index results in an answer of 0.


 
Constraints:

1 <= k <= words.length <= 10^5
1 <= words[i].length <= 10^4
words[i] consists of lowercase English letters.
The sum of words[i].length is smaller than or equal 10^5.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def longestCommonPrefix(self, words: List[str], k: int) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Class Solution:
    def longestCommonPrefix(self, words: List[str], k: int) -> List[int]:
        n = len(words)
        if k == n: return [0]*n
        # Build trie
        children = []  # list of dicts
        cnt = []
        depth = []
        # node 0 root
        children.append({})
        cnt.append(0)
        depth.append(0)
        # Insert words, store path node ids
        paths = []
        for w in words:
            node = 0
            path = []
            for ch in w:
                if ch not in children[node]:
                    children[node][ch] = len(children)
                    children.append({})
                    cnt.append(0)
                    depth.append(depth[node]+1)
                node = children[node][ch]
                path.append(node)
                cnt[node] += 1
            paths.append(path)
        # But wait cnt increments during insertion. If we increment as we go, counts are final after all insertions? Yes each word increments each node once. But if we increment during insertion of current word, subsequent insertions increment further. At end cnt is total. However when creating new node, cnt=0 then increment. Good. But need cnt for root? not needed. We increment child nodes only. Good.

But careful: If we increment cnt[node] while inserting, and later use cnt to determine count==k. Fine.

Potential issue: We need count of words with prefix node. We increment for every word path. Good.

Then compute abundant_max:
        abundant_max = 0
        for node in range(1, len(children)):
            if cnt[node] >= k+1:
                if depth[node] > abundant_max: abundant_max = depth[node]

Compute exact_count_by_depth:
        max_depth = max(depth) maybe max word length.
        exact_count = [0]*(max_depth+1)
        for node in range(1, len(children)):
            if cnt[node] == k:
                exact_count[depth[node]] += 1

Compute exact_multi_max:
        exact_multi_max = 0
        for d in range(1, max_depth+1):
            if exact_count[d] >= 2:
                exact_multi_max = d
        # Since depths increasing, final is max.

Compute singleton depths above exact_multi_max:
        singleton_depths = []
        for d in range(exact_multi_max+1, max_depth+1):
            if exact_count[d] == 1:
                singleton_depths.append(d)
        # They are increasing. Need iterate descending. Could reverse or iterate from end. Use singleton_depths_desc = singleton_depths[::-1] maybe.

But wait exact_multi_max is max depth with exact_count>=2. If exact_count[d]>=2 at some depth, exact_contribution at least d. For depths > d, exact_count can be 0 or 1. So singleton_depths above d. Good.

Now for each word, compute exact_contribution:
        ans = [abundant_max] * n? Wait answer = max(abundant_max, exact_contribution_i). Since abundant_max global same. We can initialize ans = [abundant_max]*n, then for each word compute exact_contribution and if greater update. But need if abundant_max maybe 0. Good.
        For each word i:
            path = paths[i]
            path_exact_depths = set()
            for node in path:
                if cnt[node] == k:
                    path_exact_depths.add(depth[node])
            exact_contrib = exact_multi_max
            # find first singleton depth > exact_multi_max not in path_exact_depths
            for d in singleton_depths_desc:
                if d not in path_exact_depths:
                    exact_contrib = d
                    break
            if exact_contrib > ans[i]: ans[i] = exact_contrib
        return ans

Now, let's think if answer should be length of longest common prefix among any k strings, not necessarily maximum prefix length with at least k strings sharing it? Suppose there are k strings sharing prefix length L, but maybe their LCP is longer than L? If they share prefix length L, the LCP of those k strings is at least L. The maximum over choices of LCP equals max L such that some k strings share prefix length L. Because if a choice has LCP = L*, then they share prefix length L* (and any L<=L*). Conversely, if some k strings share prefix length L, their LCP >= L, so max >= L. Thus max L exactly. Good.

Let's ensure code uses `List` imported. We'll include:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestCommonPrefix(self, words: List[str], k: int) -> List[int]:
        n = len(words)
        if k == n:
            return [0] * n
        children = [{}]
        cnt = [0]
        depth = [0]
        paths = []
        for w in words:
            node = 0
            path = []
            for ch in w:
                nxt = children[node].get(ch)
                if nxt is None:
                    nxt = len(children)
                    children[node][ch] = nxt
                    children.append({})
                    cnt.append(0)
                    depth.append(depth[node] + 1)
                node = nxt
                path.append(node)
                cnt[node] += 1
            paths.append(path)
        max_depth = max(depth) if len(depth)>1 else 0
        abundant_max = 0
        exact_count = [0] * (max_depth + 1)
        for node in range(1, len(children)):
            d = depth[node]
            c = cnt[node]
            if c >= k + 1:
                if d > abundant_max:
                    abundant_max = d
            elif c == k:
                exact_count[d] += 1
        exact_multi_max = 0
        for d in range(1, max_depth + 1):
            if exact_count[d] >= 2:
                exact_multi_max = d
        singleton_depths = []
        for d in range(exact_multi_max + 1, max_depth + 1):
            if exact_count[d] == 1:
                singleton_depths.append(d)
        singleton_depths_desc = singleton_depths[::-1]
        ans = [abundant_max] * n
        for i, path in enumerate(paths):
            path_exact = set()
            for node in path:
                if cnt[node] == k:
                    path_exact.add(depth[node])
            best = exact_multi_max
            for d in singleton_depths_desc:
                if d not in path_exact:
                    best = d
                    break
            if best > ans[i]:
                ans[i] = best
        return ans
